Outage Filename: ./20190823-Summary of the Amazon EC2 Issues in the Asia Pacific (Tokyo) Region (AP-NORTHEAST-1).txt
--
Based on the provided text, I will resume the outage for Amazon AWS.

**Outage Details:**

* **Cloud Provider:** Amazon AWS
* **Incident:** A small percentage of EC2 servers in a single Availability Zone (AZ) in the Tokyo (AP-NORTHEAST-1) Region shut down due to overheating.
* **Start Time:** 12:36 PM JST on August 23, 2019
* **Duration:** The outage lasted for approximately 3 hours and 45 minutes.

**Impacted Services and Regions/AZs:**

* Amazon EC2 in the Tokyo (AP-NORTHEAST-1) Region, specifically in a single Availability Zone.
* Amazon EBS resources in the affected area of the Availability Zone.
* Some other services, such as RDS, Redshift, ElastiCache, and Workspaces, would have seen some impact if their underlying EC2 instances were affected.

**Root Cause:**
The root cause of the outage was a control system failure that caused multiple, redundant cooling systems to fail in parts of the affected Availability Zone.

**Downtime Period:**

* The affected cooling systems were restored at 3:21 PM JST.
* Temperatures in the affected areas began to return to normal after the cooling systems were restored.
* Power was restored to the affected instances by 6:30 PM JST.
* A small number of instances and volumes hosted on hardware that was adversely affected by the loss of power and excessive heat took longer to recover.

**Resolved Time:** The outage was marked as resolved at 6:30 PM JST on August 23, 2019.  
--

I can only provide information on Amazon AWS as per the given context.

Here is the summary of the outage:

**Outage Details:**

* **Cloud Provider:** Amazon AWS
* **Services Impacted:** Direct Connect service
* **Regions Affected:** Tokyo (AP-NORTHEAST-1) Region
* **Availability Zones:** Not specified
* **Root Cause:** Failure of a subset of network devices on one of the network layers along the network path from Direct Connect edge locations to the Datacenter network in the Tokyo Region

**Outage Timeline:**

* **Start Time:** September 2, 2021 at 7:30 AM JST
* **Duration:** Approximately 5 hours and 12 minutes (from 7:30 AM JST to 12:42 PM JST)
* **Downtime Period:** The outage started with intermittent connectivity issues and elevated packet loss for Direct Connect customers connecting to the Tokyo Region. Customers started seeing recovery by 12:30 PM JST, and by 1:42 PM JST, all other forms of network connectivity were fully resolved.

**Resolution Time:**

* **Resolved Time:** September 2, 2021 at 1:42 PM JST

Note that the outage was resolved within a short period, with customers seeing recovery by 12:30 PM JST and full resolution by 1:42 PM JST.

Outage Filename: ./Azure_status_history_Microsoft_Azure_p9_2020.txt
--
Based on the provided text, I will resume the outage for Microsoft Azure.

**Outage Details:**

* **Incident Duration:** The incident lasted for approximately 6 hours and 36 minutes.
* **Start Time:** The outage started at 21:30 UTC.
* **End Time:** The outage was resolved by 03:26 UTC on February 26, 2021.
* **Impacted Services:** Front-end services for other clusters in the region also made calls to the impacted cluster's front-end service to obtain configuration. These calls were timed out due to unavailability, triggering the same unhandled failure condition and resulting crash.

**Root Cause:**

The root cause of the outage was a code defect in a version update of a component that controls network traffic routing between Azure regions. The main parameters of the new code were invoked only at production scale and scope levels, which led to the prevention of anomaly detection from occurring. This resulted in an abnormal, sudden increase in the number of unhealthy devices and forced a health validation of those devices before removing routes from the network.

**Downtime Period:**

The downtime period was approximately 6 hours and 36 minutes.

**Resolution Time:**

The outage was marked as resolved by 03:26 UTC on February 26, 2021.

**Incident Details**

* Incident started: 2024-06-12 12:06 US/Pacific Time (UTC-7)
* Incident ended: 2024-06-12 15:59 US/Pacific Time (UTC-7)
* Duration: 3 hours and 53 minutes
* Impacted regions:
	+ asia-southeast1
	+ europe-west3
	+ europe-west4
	+ us-central1
	+ us-east1
	+ us-west1

**Impacted Services**

* Vertex AI Online Prediction
* Dialogflow CX
* Agent Assist

**Root Cause of Outage**

A novel form of user request to the GenerateContent API triggered intermittent segmentation faults in the Vertex Serving Prediction API servers. An affected server would restart after the segfault, and load balancers would send user queries to other healthy API servers until the affected server returned to service.

**Remediation and Prevention**

Google engineers were alerted to the issue via internal production monitoring on 10 June 2024 at 07:35 US/Pacific Time (UTC-7). At this time there was no visible customer impact and Google engineers identified the root cause of the segmentation fault and developed a fix which they started progressively rolling out.

**Downtime Period**

The outage started at 12:06 US/Pacific Time (UTC-7) on June 12, 2024, and ended at 15:59 US/Pacific Time (UTC-7) on June 12, 2024. The downtime period was approximately 3 hours and 53 minutes.

**Duration of Outage**

The outage lasted for 3 hours and 53 minutes.

Note that the duration is calculated from the start time to the end time, not from the moment the issue started causing problems until it was resolved.

Based on the provided text, I will apply the query to IBM Cloud only.

**Incident Details**

* Incident Start: 20 June 2023 03:49 UTC
* Incident End: 20 June 2023 22:15 UTC
* Duration: 18 hours and 26 minutes
* Root Cause: Failed relay on the generator control system resulting in a soft (partial) failure scenario that impeded handover between the UPS (battery) and generator systems.

**Impacted Services**

* VPC - Impacted customers experienced an inability to access, manage, and provision VSIs. Any customer workloads running on these VSIs were impacted.
* SQL Query - SQL jobs/queries experienced long delays and eventually failures for all submitted jobs. Streaming jobs were running as normal where the impact was limited to SQL batch jobs.
* Databases - IBM Cloud Databases had an impact due to worker nodes being in critical status.
* Cloud Foundry - Impacted customers experienced issues with CF command.
* Containers Kubernetes - Multiple nodes and carriers went down.
* DNS - Service degradation experienced for appliance (custom-resolver and origin healthcheck). Customers with HA deployment configuration of custom-resolvers would not have seen any impacts. Provisioning of new appliance failed.
* VPC VPN - Impacted customers experienced VPN Gateway provisioning failures and VSI was stuck in "pending" status.
* Code Engine - Impacted customers experienced delays in creating or updating applications, due to the degraded performance of the system.

**Region and Availability Zone**

The incident occurred in Frankfurt (FRA05) Data Center and eu-de zone 3 (EU-DE-3).

**Downtime Period**

* The downtime period for each service is not explicitly stated in the text. However, it can be inferred that the outage started at 03:49 UTC and ended at 22:15 UTC.

Note: The other cloud providers mentioned in the original text (Amazon AWS, Google GCP, Microsoft Azure, Oracle OCI, Snowflake, Databricks, MongoDB, Akamai, and Cloudflare) are not included in this response as they were not identified in the provided text.

5. 21 June 2022 08:06 - Customers and service teams reported restoration of services. Incident ended.

**Additional Information**
